=== SOURCE CODE (py) ===
'''
# The class allows to split sentences, count words in a sentence, and process a text file to find the maximum word count.

import re

class SplitSentence:

    def split_sentences(self, sentences_string):
        """
        Split a string into a list of sentences. Sentences end with . or ? and with a space after that. Please note that Mr. also end with . but are not sentences.
        :param sentences_string: string, string to split
        :return:list, split sentence list
        >>> ss = SplitSentence()
        >>> ss.split_sentences("aaa aaaa. bb bbbb bbb? cccc cccc. dd ddd?")
        ['aaa aaaa.', 'bb bbbb bbb?', 'cccc cccc.', 'dd ddd?']
        """

    def count_words(self, sentence):
        """
        Count the number of words in a sentence. Note that words are separated by spaces and that punctuation marks and numbers are not counted as words.
        :param sentence:string, sentence to be counted, where words are separated by spaces
        :return:int, number of words in the sentence
        >>> ss.count_words("abc def")
        2
        """

    def process_text_file(self, sentences_string):
        """
        Given a text, return the number of words in the longest sentence
        :param sentences_string: string, undivided long sentence
        :return:int, the number of words in the longest sentence
        >>> ss.process_text_file("aaa aaaa. bb bbbb bbb? cccc ccccccc cc ccc. dd ddd?")
        4
        """
'''

import re


class SplitSentence:

    def split_sentences(self, sentences_string):
        sentences = re.split(r'(?<!\w\.\w.)(?<![A-Z][a-z]\.)(?<=\.|\?)\s', sentences_string)
        return sentences

    def count_words(self, sentence):
        sentence = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', sentence)
        words = sentence.split()
        return len(words)

    def process_text_file(self, sentences_string):
        sentences = self.split_sentences(sentences_string)
        max_count = 0
        for sentence in sentences:
            count = self.count_words(sentence)
            if count > max_count:
                max_count = count

        return max_count

```py
'''
# The class allows to split sentences, count words in a sentence, and process a text file to find the maximum word count.

import re

class SplitSentence:

    def split_sentences(self, sentences_string):
        """
        Split a string into a list of sentences. Sentences end with . or ? and with a space after that. Please note that Mr. also end with . but are not sentences.
        :param sentences_string: string, string to split
        :return:list, split sentence list
        >>> ss = SplitSentence()
        >>> ss.split_sentences("aaa aaaa. bb bbbb bbb? cccc cccc. dd ddd?")
        ['aaa aaaa.', 'bb bbbb bbb?', 'cccc cccc.', 'dd ddd?']
        """

    def count_words(self, sentence):
        """
        Count the number of words in a sentence. Note that words are separated by spaces and that punctuation marks and numbers are not counted as words.
        :param sentence:string, sentence to be counted, where words are separated by spaces
        :return:int, number of words in the sentence
        >>> ss.count_words("abc def")
        2
        """

    def process_text_file(self, sentences_string):
        """
        Given a text, return the number of words in the longest sentence
        :param sentences_string: string, undivided long sentence
        :return:int, the number of words in the longest sentence
        >>> ss.process_text_file("aaa aaaa. bb bbbb bbb? cccc ccccccc cc ccc. dd ddd?")
        4
        """
'''

import re


class SplitSentence:

    def split_sentences(self, sentences_string):
        sentences = re.split(r'(?<!\w\.\w.)(?<![A-Z][a-z]\.)(?<=\.|\?)\s', sentences_string)
        return sentences

    def count_words(self, sentence):
        sentence = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', sentence)
        words = sentence.split()
        return len(words)

    def process_text_file(self, sentences_string):
        sentences = self.split_sentences(sentences_string)
        max_count = 0
        for sentence in sentences:
            count = self.count_words(sentence)
            if count > max_count:
                max_count = count

        return max_count

```

class SplitSentence {
public:
    std::vector<std::string> split_sentences(const std::string& sentences_string);
    int count_words(const std::string& sentence);
    int process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string);
};

Implement helper functions in anonymous namespace or private static.

Need to be careful with std::isspace: takes int, should cast to unsigned char.

is_word: (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_'.

is_upper: c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z'.
is_lower: c >= 'a' && c <= 'z'.

is_space: c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v' || maybe std::isspace.

split_sentences implementation:

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }
    std::string current;
    int n = s.size();
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        char c = s[i];
        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i-1] == '.' || s[i-1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i-4]) && s[i-3] == '.' && is_word(s[i-2]) && s[i-1] != '\n') {
                    // dot in pattern matches any char except newline; s[i-1] is '.' or '?' so not newline
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }
            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i-3]) && is_lower(s[i-2]) && s[i-1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }
            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue; // skip adding the whitespace
            }
        }
        current.push_back(c);
    }
    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

But note: Python's \w in Unicode may match more than ASCII. But we assume ASCII. Could mention.

Potential issue: The negative lookbehind (?<!\w\.\w.) is a variable-length? Actually it's fixed length 4. Python's re lookbehind requires fixed length. Good.

But there is subtlety: The pattern \w\.\w. includes a dot at the end that matches any char except newline. In our condition, we check s[i-1] != '\n' but s[i-1] is '.' or '?' so always true. So we can just check the first three and s[i-1] is any char. Since s[i-1] is '.' or '?', it's fine. But to be exact, if s[i-1] were newline, split wouldn't happen because positive lookbehind requires '.' or '?'. So no issue.

Another subtlety: Python re.split with pattern that has lookbehind: The match is zero-width? Actually the pattern includes \s, so it consumes the whitespace. The lookbehinds are zero-width. Our manual consumption matches.

Now count_words: Let's implement:

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }
    std::istringstream iss(filtered);
    int count = 0;
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        ++count;
    }
    return count;
}

Note: Python re.sub removes numbers and punctuation, keeps letters and whitespace. Then split() splits on whitespace. If filtered has only whitespace, count 0. Good.

process_text_file:

int SplitSentence::process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences = split_sentences(sentences_string);
    int max_count = 0;
    for (const auto& sentence : sentences) {
        int count = count_words(sentence);
        if (count > max_count) max_count = count;
    }
    return max_count;
}

Now, C++ std::string uses char, possibly signed. We'll use char and cast to unsigned char for isspace. For is_word etc., char comparisons are fine.

Need to ensure we include <cctype> for std::isspace. But we can implement is_space manually to avoid locale issues. Let's implement:

bool is_space(char c) {
    return c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v';
}

This matches ASCII whitespace. Python \s also includes other Unicode whitespace, but input likely ASCII. If we want to be more exact, use std::isspace with unsigned char. But std::isspace in C locale returns true for these six. We'll use manual to be safe.

If there are not enough characters, the pattern cannot match, so assertion succeeds. Our check requires i>=4 and all conditions. If i<4, negative1 false. That matches.

#include <string>
#include <vector>
#include <sstream>

namespace {

bool is_space(char c) {
    return c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v';
}

bool is_word(char c) {
    return (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
}

bool is_upper(char c) {
    return c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z';
}

bool is_lower(char c) {
    return c >= 'a' && c <= 'z';
}

} // namespace

class SplitSentence {
public:
    std::vector<std::string> split_sentences(const std::string& sentences_string);
    int count_words(const std::string& sentence);
    int process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string);
};

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }
    std::string current;
    const int n = static_cast<int>(s.size());
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        char c = s[i];
        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i - 1] == '.' || s[i - 1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i - 4]) && s[i - 3] == '.' && is_word(s[i - 2])) {
                    // The final dot of \w\.\w. matches s[i-1] (which is '.' or '?').
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }
            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i - 3]) && is_lower(s[i - 2]) && s[i - 1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }
            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue;
            }
        }
        current.push_back(c);
    }
    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }
    std::istringstream iss(filtered);
    int count = 0;
    std::string word;
    while (iss >> word) {
        ++count;
    }
    return count;
}

int SplitSentence::process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences = split_sentences(sentences_string);
    int max_count = 0;
    for (const std::string& sentence : sentences) {
        int count = count_words(sentence);
        if (count > max_count) {
            max_count = count;
        }
    }
    return max_count;
}

Implementation:

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }
    std::string current;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i) {
        char c = s[i];
        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i - 1] == '.' || s[i - 1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i - 4]) && s[i - 3] == '.' && is_word(s[i - 2])) {
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }
            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i - 3]) && is_lower(s[i - 2]) && s[i - 1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }
            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue;
            }
        }
        current.push_back(c);
    }
    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

Using size_t, `i - 4` when i>=4 is safe. Good.

Now, count_words manual:

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }
    int count = 0;
    bool in_word = false;
    for (char c : filtered) {
        if (is_space(c)) {
            in_word = false;
        } else if (!in_word) {
            ++count;
            in_word = true;
        }
    }
    return count;
}

Final code:

```cpp
#include <string>
#include <vector>

namespace {

bool is_space(char c) {
    return c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v';
}

bool is_word(char c) {
    return (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') ||
           (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
}

bool is_upper(char c) {
    return c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z';
}

bool is_lower(char c) {
    return c >= 'a' && c <= 'z';
}

} // namespace

class SplitSentence {
public:
    std::vector<std::string> split_sentences(const std::string& sentences_string);
    int count_words(const std::string& sentence);
    int process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string);
};

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }

    std::string current;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i) {
        char c = s[i];

        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i - 1] == '.' || s[i - 1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i - 4]) && s[i - 3] == '.' && is_word(s[i - 2])) {
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }

            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i - 3]) && is_lower(s[i - 2]) && s[i - 1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }

            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue;
            }
        }

        current.push_back(c);
    }

    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }

    int count = 0;
    bool in_word = false;
    for (char c : filtered) {
        if (is_space(c)) {
            in_word = false;
        } else if (!in_word) {
            ++count;
            in_word = true;
        }
    }

    return count;
}

int SplitSentence::process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences = split_sentences(sentences_string);
    int max_count = 0;
    for (const std::string& sentence : sentences) {
        int count = count_words(sentence);
        if (count > max_count) {
            max_count = count;
        }
    }
    return max_count;
}
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
. The user asked for a translation of Python code to C++. The final response includes a C++ code block. I need to ensure the code is correct and matches the Python behavior. The code seems to implement the class with methods split_sentences, count_words, process_text_file. It uses manual scanning instead of regex lookbehind. I should verify the logic.

Let's review the C++ code in the response:

```cpp
#include <string>
#include <vector>

namespace {

bool is_space(char c) {
    return c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v';
}

bool is_word(char c) {
    return (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') ||
           (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
}

bool is_upper(char c) {
    return c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z';
}

bool is_lower(char c) {
    return c >= 'a' && c <= 'z';
}

} // namespace

class SplitSentence {
public:
    std::vector<std::string> split_sentences(const std::string& sentences_string) const;
    int count_words(const std::string& sentence) const;
    int process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) const;
};

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) const {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }

    std::string current;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i) {
        char c = s[i];

        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i - 1] == '.' || s[i - 1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i - 4]) && s[i - 3] == '.' && is_word(s[i - 2])) {
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }

            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i - 3]) && is_lower(s[i - 2]) && s[i - 1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }

            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue;
            }
        }

        current.push_back(c);
    }

    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) const {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }

    int count = 0;
    bool in_word = false;
    for (char c : filtered) {
        if (is_space(c)) {
            in_word = false;
        } else if (!in_word) {
            ++count;
            in_word = true;
        }
    }

    return count;
}

int SplitSentence::process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) const {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences = split_sentences(sentences_string);
    int max_count = 0;
    for (const std::string& sentence : sentences) {
        int count = count_words(sentence);
        if (count > max_count) {
            max_count = count;
        }
    }
    return max_count;
}
```

But wait: The pattern `\w\.\w.` has a trailing dot that matches any char except newline. Since `s[i-1]` is '.' or '?', it's not newline. So the condition is correct.

The second negative lookbehind `(?<![A-Z][a-z]\.)` is 3 characters. They check `is_upper(s[i-3]) && is_lower(s[i-2]) && s[i-1] == '.'`. Correct.

=== TRANSLATED CODE (cpp) ===
#include <string>
#include <vector>

namespace {

bool is_space(char c) {
    return c == ' ' || c == '\t' || c == '\n' || c == '\r' || c == '\f' || c == '\v';
}

bool is_word(char c) {
    return (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') ||
           (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';
}

bool is_upper(char c) {
    return c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z';
}

bool is_lower(char c) {
    return c >= 'a' && c <= 'z';
}

} // namespace

class SplitSentence {
public:
    std::vector<std::string> split_sentences(const std::string& sentences_string) const;
    int count_words(const std::string& sentence) const;
    int process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) const;
};

std::vector<std::string> SplitSentence::split_sentences(const std::string& s) const {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences;
    if (s.empty()) {
        sentences.push_back("");
        return sentences;
    }

    std::string current;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < s.size(); ++i) {
        char c = s[i];

        if (is_space(c) && i > 0 && (s[i - 1] == '.' || s[i - 1] == '?')) {
            bool negative1 = false;
            if (i >= 4) {
                if (is_word(s[i - 4]) && s[i - 3] == '.' && is_word(s[i - 2])) {
                    negative1 = true;
                }
            }

            bool negative2 = false;
            if (i >= 3) {
                if (is_upper(s[i - 3]) && is_lower(s[i - 2]) && s[i - 1] == '.') {
                    negative2 = true;
                }
            }

            if (!negative1 && !negative2) {
                sentences.push_back(current);
                current.clear();
                continue;
            }
        }

        current.push_back(c);
    }

    sentences.push_back(current);
    return sentences;
}

int SplitSentence::count_words(const std::string& sentence) const {
    std::string filtered;
    for (char c : sentence) {
        if ((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || is_space(c)) {
            filtered.push_back(c);
        }
    }

    int count = 0;
    bool in_word = false;
    for (char c : filtered) {
        if (is_space(c)) {
            in_word = false;
        } else if (!in_word) {
            ++count;
            in_word = true;
        }
    }

    return count;
}

int SplitSentence::process_text_file(const std::string& sentences_string) const {
    std::vector<std::string> sentences = split_sentences(sentences_string);
    int max_count = 0;
    for (const std::string& sentence : sentences) {
        int count = count_words(sentence);
        if (count > max_count) {
            max_count = count;
        }
    }
    return max_count;
}
